# Decision and Progress Log

- Status: Active
- Documentation entry point: [repository documentation map](../../README.md#documentation-map)
- Implementation sequence: [implementation plan](../IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md)

## Purpose

This stable-path log records reversible execution decisions and evidence-linked progress while the implementation plan is carried out. It does not replace requirements, accepted architecture decision records, decision gates, implementation tasks, the root current-status summary, or repository and runtime evidence.

Architectural decisions must use the ADR workflow. A DPL entry cannot change product scope, optional disposition, accepted architecture, or gate status. Completed work must link to reproducible evidence before it is reported as implemented or passing.

| DPL-DEC-015 | 2026-08-11 | Recorded | DPL-DEC-014; ADR-0010; TASK-003 | Add privacy-minimized, non-blocking metrics to worker-first implementation runs. Project hooks record sanitized subagent lifecycle events; the primary coordinator records semantic events and owns workflow, cycle, and lease correlation. Count a correction only when additional work follows a rejected handoff or actionable review finding, a false Red only when the coordinator rejects a Red barrier, and a regression only after triage proves that the current change broke a stable identified check which previously passed. Derive elapsed time from validated paired timestamps and record tokens only when the runtime supplies exact values. | This separates automatic timing from coordinator-only semantic classification, permits out-of-order concurrent hook writes without a shared log lock, makes exact logical replays idempotent, and exposes conflicting events, invalid transitions, and missing lifecycle or token coverage instead of fabricating precision. Metrics remain workflow observations: they cannot accept Red, authorize Green, replace implementation evidence, alter a reviewer verdict, become a delivery gate, or block task closure. The [metrics policy](../../.codex/agent-flow-metrics.md) owns definitions, operation, privacy, and limitations. This reversible workflow configuration does not start or modify TASK-003 product execution and does not invalidate its reviewed implementation semantics. |
| DPL-DEC-016 | 2026-08-12 | Recorded | DPL-DEC-014; DPL-DEC-015; ADR-0010; TASK-003 | Make worker path leases automatically verifiable at handoff. This blocking control remains separate from DPL-DEC-015's non-blocking metrics and adds no agent, hook, product dependency, task start, gate resolution, architecture approval, or implementation evidence. |

The coordinator may append relevant context and workers may inspect additional repository files, but neither action expands a binding assignment field. | DPL-DEC-021 | 2026-08-12 | Recorded | DPL-DEC-020; TASK-003; NFR-001, NFR-003; ADR-0002, ADR-0011, ADR-0014; HS-001, HS-006, HS-007, HS-017, HS-019 | Name the private root `rick-and-morty-explorer` and its workspaces `@rick-and-morty/web`, `@rick-and-morty/api`, and `@rick-and-morty/shared`. | DPL-DEC-022 | 2026-08-12 | Recorded | DPL-DEC-020, DPL-DEC-021; TASK-003; ADR-0010, ADR-0011; HS-006, HS-007, HS-019 | Make `npm run test:smoke:lifecycle` the one lifecycle-matrix command around the exact `npm run test:smoke` child. It runs six bounded cases: normal completion; an owned listener on web smoke port `4173` for startup-conflict failure; test-only expected-heading override for assertion failure; a repository-owned never-ready API fixture for readiness timeout; controller cancellation after both owned URLs are ready; and controller interruption while API readiness is pending. After every case it proves ports `4173` and `4174` bindable. The controller may terminate only its recorded smoke child tree: Windows uses forced `taskkill /PID <owned-pid> /T /F`, while POSIX uses the owned process group. Report cancellation and interruption as forced owned-tree termination on Windows, never as graceful `SIGINT` or `SIGTERM`. | Playwright documents multiple owned web servers, `reuseExistingServer: false`, bounded startup, and forceful default process-group cleanup, and explicitly says graceful `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM` configuration is ignored on Windows. The selected triggers are deterministic, do not kill an unknown port owner, preserve the primary failure, and give cancellation and startup interruption distinct observable boundaries without amending ADR-0011. The same command still requires runtime evidence in both Windows and an already supplied continuous-integration environment before closure; no CI route is selected or created by this decision. |

Decision-oriented R3 retains mandatory analysis and pre-draft review; answer-only R3 uses primary synthesis unless an analyst trigger remains and always receives complete fresh final review. Route every terminal role result explicitly, keep no-ExecPlan policy research in the coordinating record, require fresh-session discovery for new role definitions, and retain the primary as sole authoritative writer. | Verification showed that a model and effort pinned in a custom-agent TOML cannot be replaced by the documented ordinary-role override; a separate role makes the Sol route executable. The previous R3 contract also assumed candidates, an ExecPlan, drafting, and a repository write even when the requested result was only an answer. The corrected split removes that false requirement, follows the official balanced medium baseline for ordinary research, preserves maximum-effort critical review in a research-specific role, and keeps the worker-first implementation guide, write-lease guard, implementation workers, and implementation reviewers unchanged. No product task, ADR, dependency, behavior, gate, or implementation evidence changes. |

## Conventions

- Assign stable execution-decision IDs in the form `DPL-DEC-001`; never reuse or silently rewrite an ID.
- Use ISO 8601 dates in the form `YYYY-MM-DD`.
- Use `Recorded`, `Superseded`, or `Rejected` for decision status and link a superseding entry.
- Reference only directly affected requirement, acceptance, deliverable, ADR, gate, specification, hard-specification, and task IDs.
- Keep this file at its stable path; append chronology instead of moving or renaming the global log.
- Link progress claims to repository or runtime evidence and preserve the distinction between intent and proof.
